4   Commerzbank Interim Report as at 30 June 2018




       Performance highlights
       1 January to 30 June 2018

                              Key statements

                              • Commerzbank made further progress with the implementation of its “Commerzbank 4.0” strategy in the first
                                 half of 2018. With the agreement on the sale of its Equity Markets & Commodities business to Société
                                 Générale, Commerzbank reached a further milestone in the simplification of its business model. In the
                                 Private and Small-Business Customers segment, customer growth continued at a slightly reduced pace. In
                                 the Corporate Clients segment, the volume of lending continued to rise.


                              • The operating result for the first half of 2018 amounted to €689m, compared with €505m in the prior-year
                                 period. Consolidated profit attributable to Commerzbank shareholders was €533m, compared with €–414m
                                 in the prior-year period.


                              • The Group risk result was €–161m, with the quality of the credit portfolio continuing to make a positive
                                 impact; the non-performing loans (NPL) ratio was 0.9%. Operating expenses increased, especially owing to
                                 higher investment in digitalisation and growth, and also due to higher costs for regulatory projects and
                                 levies, such as Deposit Protection Fund and Polish banking tax.


                              • The Common Equity Tier 1 ratio was 13.0%; the leverage ratio was 4.5% (fully phased-in).

                              • The operating return on equity was 6.1%, compared with 4.3% in the prior-year period. The return on
                                 equity based on consolidated profit or loss (less intangible assets; return on tangible equity) was 4.3%,
                                 compared with –3.2% the year before. The cost/income ratio rose to 81.3%.


                              • For the financial year 2018, Commerzbank is planning to distribute a dividend as announced.



                              Development of Commerzbank shares

                              Events on international stock markets were defined by a host of geopolitical events in the first six months of
                              2018, including the escalating trade tensions between the USA and China and Europe, elections in Russia, Italy
                              and Turkey, the formation of a grand coalition in the German parliament and the ongoing political tensions in
                              the Middle East and North Korea. Volatility shot up at the end of January, afflicting the positive start to the year
                              of the German equity market, which then suffered considerable setbacks as risk aversion rose. German
                              government bonds were in particular demand and saw their yields fall. The uncertainties were also apparent in
                              the major currencies, with the US dollar stronger against the euro. In an environment of falling rate
                              expectations, European banks and cyclical stocks in particular saw price falls, in some cases sharp ones. In the
                              first six months of 2018, the EURO-STOXX Banks Index fell by –15.4%, while the Commerzbank share lost
                              –34.3% compared with its level at start of the year. This trend is largely due to the fact that general expectations
                              of a rate hike have weakened since year-end, an aspect to which investors attach greater-than-average
                              significance for the profitability of Commerzbank. The takeover hopes that were so apparent last year also
                              declined sharply in the first half of 2018.

Important business policy events

Key milestone in implementing the Commerzbank 4.0 strategy
On     3 July    Commerzbank           entered      into    an     agreement        with    Société   Générale    to   sell   the     Equity
Markets & Commodities (EMC) division. The Commerzbank EMC business includes issuing and market making
in structured trading and investment products, the established ComStage brand for exchange-traded funds
(ETFs) and the associated leading platform for ETF market making. The equity capital markets (ECM) business,
equity trading and equity sales brokerage are not part of this transaction. The same applies to the business in
hedging products for commodity risk. These activities will remain at Commerzbank as part of the strategic
customer business.
     The transaction will see trading books, customer business, employees and parts of the IT infrastructure
transferred. EMC front office staff and some employees in downstream units are to move to Société Générale.
Transfer of the trading books and the corresponding impact on the balance sheet and income will take place in
stages, probably starting at the end of 2018. It is therefore likely that EMC revenue will cease to appear in the
income statement over the course of 2019. The reduction in expenses associated with EMC should remove at
least €200m from the Commerzbank cost base by the end of 2020 and contribute to achieving the cost target set
out in 2016 as part of the Commerzbank 4.0 strategy.
     The transaction still requires consultation with the relevant tax authorities, the consent of other authorities
and employee representative bodies involved and completion of the legal documentation.

Risk-oriented overall bank                                                        Commerzbank monitors risk-bearing capacity (RBC) using a
                                                                             gone concern approach which seeks primarily to protect unsub-
management                                                                   ordinated lenders. This objective should be achieved even in the
                                                                             event of extraordinarily high losses from an unlikely extreme
Commerzbank defines risk as the danger of possible losses or                 event. The gone concern analysis is supplemented here by
profits foregone due to internal or external factors. In risk                elements aimed at ensuring the institution’s continuing existence
management, we normally distinguish between quantifiable and                 (going concern perspective).
non-quantifiable types of risk. Quantifiable risks are those to which             In addition, risk-bearing capacity is assessed using macro-
a value can normally be attached in financial statements or in               economic stress scenarios. The Group Risk Report 2017 provides
regulatory capital requirements, while non-quantifiable risks                further details on the methodology used.
include compliance and reputational risk.                                         The monitoring and management by means of risk-bearing
                                                                             capacity is carried out monthly at Group level. Risk-bearing
                                                                             capacity is deemed to be assured as long as the RBC ratio is higher
Risk management organisation                                                 than 100%. In the year to date, the RBC ratio has consistently been
                                                                             above 100% and stood at 192% as at 30 June 2018. The decrease
Commerzbank regards risk management as a task for the whole                  in risk coverage potential during the first half of 2018 was mainly
bank. The Chief Risk Officer (CRO) is responsible for developing             due to the introduction of IFRS 9, time-to-maturity effects of
and implementing the Group’s risk policy guidelines for                      subordinated capital and the market-related developments in the
quantifiable risks, laid down by the Board of Managing Directors,            Public Finance portfolio. The RBC ratio remains at a high level.
as well as for measuring these risks. The CRO regularly reports to
the Board of Managing Directors and the Risk Committee of the                    Risk-bearing capacity Group | €bn                   30.6.2018       31.12.2017

     fair value option for its own structured issues in order to avoid an
     accounting mismatch between hedging derivatives measured at

     • Internal rate of return (IRR):                                           recovery rate assumption) and hence a lower survival
        The IRR is defined as the discount rate that sets the net present       probability. There is a relationship over time between default

        The credit spread is the yield spread (premium or discount)             recovery rate.
        between securities that are identical in all respects except for        In practice, market participants use market spreads to
        their respective credit quality. The credit spread represents the       determine implied default probabilities. Estimates of default

        creditworthiness, and vice versa for narrower (lower) credit

     • Interest rate-forex (IR-FX) correlation:
        The IR-FX correlation is relevant for the pricing of exotic
        interest rate swaps involving the exchange of funding
        instruments in one currency and an exotic structured leg that is
        usually based on the development of two government bond
        yields in different currencies. Consensus market data for longer
        durations are not observable for certain exotic interest
        products. For example, CMT yields for US government bonds
        with a duration of more than ten years are not observable.

liquidity ratio. It is a measure of the near-term solvency of the          risk concentrations in funding can be recognised in a timely man-
Bank under a predetermined stress scenario. Based on the re-               ner and mitigated through suitable measures. This also applies to
quirements of the Basel Committee, the EU Commission set out               payment obligations in foreign currencies. The Bank also mitigates
the legal foundation for the LCR in the Capital Requirements               concentrations through the continuous use of the broadly diversi-
Regulation (CRR) and in Regulation (EU) No. 575/2013, in con-              fied sources of funding available to it, particularly in the form of
junction with Delegated Regulation EU/2015/61 (D-REG).                     diverse customer deposits and capital market instruments.

in this form since 30 September 2016. The CRR stipulates that the          located in Frankfurt, London, New York and Singapore.

Reservation regarding forward-looking statements
This interim report contains forward-looking statements on Commerzbank’s business and earnings performance, which are based
upon our current plans, estimates, forecasts and expectations. The statements entail risks and uncertainties, as there are a variety of
factors which influence our business and to a great extent lie beyond our sphere of influence. Above all, these include the economic
situation, the state of the financial markets worldwide and possible loan losses. Actual results and developments may, therefore,
diverge considerably from our current assumptions, which, for this reason, are valid only at the time of publication. We undertake no
obligation to revise our forward-­looking statements in the light of either new information or unexpected events.
